\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)^{\top}(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})=\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})^{\top}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})=t_{2}^{L}L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),

and
L(L-1)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})t_{2}^{L-2}\|t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}=L(L-1)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})t_{2}^{L}\geq t_{2}^{L}L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}).

Therefore, we assume
t_{1}>0
. Now, define
t=t_{2}/t_{1}
, then
eq.

23
can be written as

\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}(t{\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)^{\top}({\mathbf{w}}-t{\mathbf{w}}_{*})-L(L-1)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})t^{L-2}\|{\mathbf{w}}-t{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 0.

(24)

We will show that the above inequality is true for all
t\geq 1
to prove the lemma. We will proceed by considering two cases:
L=2
and
L>2
.

Case 1 (
L=2
):
Since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
is a
\Delta
-second-order KKT point, from Lemma
21
we know

\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{w}}_{*},\text{ and }\Delta+{\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),\forall{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\perp}.

(25)

For the sake of brevity, we define
\lambda_{*}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
and
b_{*}={\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}
here onward in this proof. Using Lemma
20
, homogeneity and
\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}=1
, the LHS of
eq.

24
can be written as

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})+2t^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}-2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})(1+t^{2}-2t{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})+\lambda_{*}t^{2}-\lambda_{*}t{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}-\lambda_{*}(1+t^{2}-2t{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\lambda_{*}-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\lambda_{*}t{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}},

where the first equality uses
eq.

25
, and simplifying the first equality leads to the second. Since
{\mathbf{w}}
has unit norm, we define
{\mathbf{w}}=({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})/\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}
, where
{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\perp}
. Putting this choice of
{\mathbf{w}}
in the above equation gives us

\frac{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})}-\lambda_{*}-\frac{t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}+\frac{\lambda_{*}t{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}.

To complete the proof, we will show that the above quantity is less than zero for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Using Lemma
22
and
\|{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}=1
, the above quantity becomes

\frac{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\epsilon^{2}b_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})}-\lambda_{*}-\frac{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+o(\epsilon^{2})}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}+\frac{\lambda_{*}t}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}},

which can be further simplified to get

\frac{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon^{2}b_{*}}{(1+\epsilon^{2})}-\lambda_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2}).

Since
1/(1+\epsilon^{2})=1-\epsilon^{2}+o(\epsilon^{2})
, the above quantity can be written as

\lambda_{*}(1-\epsilon^{2})+\epsilon^{2}b_{*}-\lambda_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2})=\epsilon^{2}(b_{*}-\lambda_{*})+o(\epsilon^{2})\leq-\Delta\epsilon^{2}+o(\epsilon^{2}).

Since
\Delta>0
, the proof is complete.

Case 2 (
L>2
):
Since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
is a
\Delta-
second-order KKT point, from Lemma
21
we have

\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{w}}_{*},\text{ and }\Delta+{\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}\leq L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),\forall{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\perp}.

(26)

For the sake of brevity, we define
\lambda_{*}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
and
b_{*}={\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}
here onward in this proof. Using Lemma
20
, homogeneity and
\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}=1
, the LHS of
eq.

24
can be written as

\displaystyle L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})+Lt^{L}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})-t^{L-1}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}

\displaystyle-t^{L-2}L(L-1)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})(1+t^{2}-2t{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})

\displaystyle=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})+\lambda_{*}t^{L}-\lambda_{*}t^{L-1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}-\lambda_{*}t^{L-2}(L-1)(1+t^{2}-2t{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})

\displaystyle=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\lambda_{*}((L-1)t^{L-2}+(L-2)t^{L})-t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}},

where the first equality uses
eq.

26
, and simplifying the first equality leads to the second. Define
{\mathbf{w}}=({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})/\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}
, where
{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\perp}
. Putting this choice of
{\mathbf{w}}
in the above equation gives us

\frac{L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})^{L/2}}-\lambda_{*}((L-1)t^{L-2}+(L-2)t^{L})-\frac{t\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}}{({1+\epsilon^{2}})^{(L-1)/2}}+\frac{(2L-3)\lambda_{*}t^{L-1}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}.

To complete the proof, we will show that the above quantity is less than zero for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Using Lemma
22
and
\|{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}=1
, we further get

\frac{\lambda_{*}+\frac{L\epsilon^{2}}{2}b_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})^{L/2}}-\lambda_{*}((L-1)t^{L-2}+(L-2)t^{L})-\frac{t\lambda_{*}+\frac{t(L-2)\epsilon^{2}}{2}b_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2})}{({1+\epsilon^{2}})^{(L-1)/2}}+\frac{\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}.

Since
1/(1+\epsilon^{2})^{k}=1-k\epsilon^{2}+o(\epsilon^{2})
, the above quantity can be written as

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}\left(1-\frac{L\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)+\frac{L\epsilon^{2}b_{*}}{2}-\lambda_{*}((L-1)t^{L-2}+(L-2)t^{L})-t\lambda_{*}\left(1-\frac{(L-1)\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{t(L-2)b_{*}\epsilon^{2}}{2}+\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)+o(\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{*}(1-(L-1)t^{L-2}-(L-2)t^{L}-t+(2L-3)t^{L-1})

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(-L\lambda_{*}+Lb_{*}+t(L-1)\lambda_{*}-t(L-2)b_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1})+o(\epsilon^{2}).

Now, define
a(t)=(1+(L-2)t^{L-1}-(L-1)t^{L-2})
, then

(1-t)a(t)=1-(L-1)t^{L-2}-(L-2)t^{L}-t+(2L-3)t^{L-1}.

Also, note that
a(1)=0
, and
a^{\prime}(t)=(L-2)(L-1)(t^{L-2}-t^{L-3})\geq 0,
for all
t\geq 1.
Hence,
a(t)\geq 0
and an increasing function, for all
t\geq 1
.

Next, let
b(t)=-L\lambda_{*}+Lb_{*}+t(L-1)\lambda_{*}-t(L-2)b_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}
. Thus, our goal is to that show for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
,

(1-t)a(t)\lambda_{*}+b(t)\epsilon^{2}/2+o(\epsilon^{2})\leq 0.

(27)

We first consider the case when
t\in[1,L/(L-2)]
. In this case, since
a(t)\geq 0
, for all
t\geq 1
, we have
(1-t)a(t)\leq 0
. Next

\displaystyle b(t)

\displaystyle=-L\lambda_{*}+(L-t(L-2))b_{*}+t(L-1)\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}

\displaystyle\leq-L\lambda_{*}+(L-t(L-2))(\lambda_{*}-\Delta)+t(L-1)\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}

\displaystyle=-t(L-2)\lambda_{*}+t(L-1)\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}-(L-t(L-2))\Delta

\displaystyle\leq t\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{*}(2L-3)t^{L-1}\leq t\lambda_{*}(4-2L)\leq\lambda_{*}(4-2L),

where the first inequality follows from
eq.

26
, and the second uses
\Delta>0
. The last two inequalities are true since
t\geq 1.
Therefore,

(1-t)a(t)\lambda_{*}+b(t)\epsilon^{2}/2+o(\epsilon^{2})\leq\lambda_{*}(4-2L)\epsilon^{2}+o(\epsilon^{2}).

Since
L>2
, the above inequality implies
eq.

27
is true for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.

We next consider
t\geq L/(L-2)
. Define
g(t)=(1-t)a(t)\lambda_{*}+b(t)\epsilon^{2}/2
. From the above discussion, we know

g(L/(L-2))\leq\lambda_{*}(4-2L)\epsilon^{2}.

Now, if we can show that for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
,
g(t)\leq g(L/(L-2))
, for all
t\geq L/(L-2)
, then

(1-t)a(t)\lambda_{*}+b(t)\epsilon^{2}/2+o(\epsilon^{2})\leq\lambda_{*}(4-2L)\epsilon^{2}+o(\epsilon^{2}),\text{ for all }t\geq L/(L-2).

Thus,
eq.

27
is true for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. To complete the proof, we next show that
g^{\prime}(t)\leq 0
, for all
t\geq L/(L-2)
and all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Note that

g^{\prime}(t)=(1-t)a^{\prime}(t)\lambda_{*}-a(t)\lambda_{*}+b^{\prime}(t)\epsilon^{2}/2.

Since
a^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
and
t\geq 1
,
(1-t)a^{\prime}(t)\lambda_{*}\leq 0
. Further,
a^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
implies
a(t)\geq a(L/(L-2))
, for all
t\geq L/(L-2)
. Since
a(L/L-2)=(1+(L/L-2)^{L-2})\geq 1
, we have that

\displaystyle-a(t)\lambda_{*}+b^{\prime}(t)\epsilon^{2}/2

\displaystyle=-a(t)\lambda_{*}+((L-1)\lambda_{*}-(L-2)b_{*}-(2L-3)(L-1)\lambda_{*}t^{L-2})\epsilon^{2}/2

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{*}-(L-2)b_{*}\epsilon^{2}/2+(L-1)\lambda_{*}(1-(2L-3)t^{L-2})\epsilon^{2}/2

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{*}-(L-2)b_{*}\epsilon^{2}/2\leq 0,

where the first inequality uses
a(t)\geq a(L/(L-2))\geq 1
. The second inequality uses
L>2
and
t\geq 1
. The last inequality is true for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Thus,
g^{\prime}(t)\leq 0.

∎

Lemma 24
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is
L-
homogeneous, where
L\geq 2
, and
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
is a
\Delta-
second-order positive KKT point of
eq.

4
. Then there exists a sufficiently small
\gamma>0
such that for all unit-norm vectors
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma
the following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}){\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0,\text{ and }

(28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\frac{\Delta}{4}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 0.

(29)

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{w}}=({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})/\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}
, where
{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\perp}
. Putting this
{\mathbf{w}}
in
eq.

28
gives us

\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})^{(L-1)/2}}-\frac{L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}}){\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})^{(L+1)/2}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}(2-2{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}).

(30)

To complete the proof, we will show that the above quantity is greater than zero for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Note that, for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
,

{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}={1}/{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}=1-{\epsilon^{2}}/{2}+o(\epsilon^{2}),

and from Lemma
22
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\frac{(L-2)\epsilon^{2}}{2}{\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}+o(\epsilon^{2})\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})=\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}{\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}+o(\epsilon^{2}).

Let
\lambda_{*}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
,
b_{*}={\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}
, then
eq.

30
can be simplified to get

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}\left(1-\frac{(L-1)\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)+\frac{(L-2)\epsilon^{2}}{2}b_{*}+o(\epsilon^{2})-\lambda_{*}\left(1-\frac{(L+1)\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)-\frac{L\epsilon^{2}}{2}b_{*}-\frac{\Delta\epsilon^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\left(-(L-1)\lambda_{*}+(L-2)b_{*}+(L+1)\lambda_{*}-Lb_{*}-\Delta\right)+o(\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\left(2\lambda_{*}-2b_{*}-\Delta\right)+o(\epsilon^{2})\geq\Delta\epsilon^{2}/2+o(\epsilon^{2}).

The last inequality uses
b_{*}\leq\lambda_{*}-\Delta
. Since
\Delta>0
, the above inequality proves
eq.

28
.

Similarly,
eq.

29
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}+\epsilon{\mathbf{b}})}{(1+\epsilon^{2})^{L/2}}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\frac{\Delta(2-2{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{4}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left(1-\frac{L\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}{\mathbf{b}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{b}}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\frac{\Delta\epsilon^{2}}{4}+o(\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(b_{*}-L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\Delta/2)+o(\epsilon^{2})\leq\frac{-\Delta\epsilon^{2}}{4}+o(\epsilon^{2}).

The last inequality uses
b_{*}\leq\lambda_{*}-\Delta
. Since
\Delta>0
, the above inequality proves
eq.

29
.
∎

A.2
Gradient Flow Dynamics of NCF Near a KKT point

In this subsection, we describe the gradient flow dynamics of NCF when initialized near a second-order positive KKT point.

Lemma 25
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is
2-
homogeneous, and
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
is a
\Delta-
second-order positive KKT point of the constrained NCF in
eq.

4
. Let
{\mathbf{w}}(t)
be the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{w}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{0},

where
\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{2}=1.
There exists a sufficiently small
\gamma>0
such that if
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{0}>1-\gamma
, then

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-e^{-t\Delta}\gamma,\forall t\geq 0,\text{ and }{\mathbf{w}}(t)={\mathbf{g}}(t)e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},

(31)

where
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}\in[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
, for some
\kappa_{2}\geq\kappa_{1}>0
.

Proof.

We chose a sufficiently small
\gamma>0
such that

\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\geq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})>0\text{ and }{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}){\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0,

(32)

for all unit-norm vector
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}>1-\gamma
. The above conditions are possible since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})>0
and due to Lemma
24
.

Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{0}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}>1-\gamma
, we have
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})>0
. From
(
Kumar and Haupt, 2024
, Lemma C.4)
,
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}\geq\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{2}>0,
for all
t\geq 0
. We next show that
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}>1-\gamma
, for all
t\geq 0
. For the sake of contradiction, suppose
\overline{T}>0
be the smallest time such that
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})/\|{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}=1-\gamma.
Then, for all
t\in[0,\overline{T})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}-2{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})-2{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\Delta\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\geq 0,

(33)

where the first inequality follows from
eq.

32
. The above equation implies

\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}\right)\geq\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)>1-\gamma,

which leads to a contradiction. Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}>1-\gamma
, for all
t\geq 0
, from
eq.

33
we have

\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\leq e^{-t\Delta}\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)\leq e^{-t\Delta}\gamma,

which proves the first part of
eq.

31
. Now, if
{\mathbf{w}}(t)={\mathbf{g}}(t)e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}
, then

\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}})e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})=\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}=\dot{{\mathbf{g}}}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}+2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{g}}(t)e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},

which implies

\dot{{\mathbf{g}}}+2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}){\mathbf{g}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}).

Multiplying the above equation by
{\mathbf{g}}^{\top}
from the left, we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{g}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}+2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|{\mathbf{g}}\|_{2}^{2}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}})=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}/\|{\mathbf{g}}\|_{2})\|{\mathbf{g}}\|_{2}^{2},

which implies

\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{2}e^{4\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(s)\|_{2})-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)ds}.

Since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2})=\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2})\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),
for all
t\geq 0
, we have

\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{2}:=\kappa_{2}.

Next, since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is locally Lipschitz, there exists a
\mu>0
such that, for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2})=|\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2})|\leq\mu\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2},

therefore, for
t\geq 0
, using the above equation and first part of
eq.

31
, we get

\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{g}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(s)\|_{2})-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)ds}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{t}-\mu\|{\mathbf{g}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{g}}(s)\|_{2}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}-\mu\sqrt{\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}-\mu\sqrt{2-2{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\geq-\mu\sqrt{2\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s\Delta/2}ds\geq-2\mu\sqrt{2\gamma}/\Delta.

Hence,
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{2}e^{-8\mu\sqrt{2\gamma}/\Delta}:=\kappa_{1},
which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 26
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is
L-
homogeneous, where
L>2
, and
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
is a
\Delta-
second-order positive KKT point of
eq.

4
. Let
{\mathbf{w}}(t)
be the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{w}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{0},

where
\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{2}=1.
There exists a sufficiently small
\gamma>0
such that if
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{0}>1-\gamma
, then
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})>0
and there exists a finite
T>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}=\infty,\text{ where }T\in\left[\frac{1}{L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\frac{1}{L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})}\right],\text{ and }

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-\gamma(1-t/T)^{\frac{\Delta}{L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Further, for all
t\in[0,T)
,
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}
is an increasing function and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{w}}(t)=\frac{{\mathbf{g}}(t)}{(T-t)^{1/(L-2)}},

where
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}
is a decreasing function for all
t\in[0,T)
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{L-2}=T,\text{ and }\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}={1}/{(L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}))}.

Proof.

We begin by choosing a sufficiently small
\gamma>0
such that

\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\geq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})>0,\text{ and }{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}){\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0,

(34)

for all unit norm vector
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}\geq 1-\gamma
. The above conditions are possible since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})>0
and due to Lemma
24
.

Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{0}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}>1-\gamma
, we have
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})>0
. From
(
Kumar and Haupt, 2025
, Lemma 13)
,
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}\geq\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{2}>0,
for all
t\geq 0
. Next, we show that
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}
becomes unbounded at some finite time. For this, define

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}})\coloneqq\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}}\right)=\frac{\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}},\text{ then }\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}}.

Now, note that

\frac{d\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}})}{dt}=\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}^{\top}\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}})=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}}\geq 0,

which implies
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}}(t))
increases with time. Hence,

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})=L\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}})\geq L\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})\geq 0,

(35)

which implies
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}
is an increasing function, for all
t\geq 0
, and

\frac{d\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}}{dt}\geq L\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-1}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0}).

Taking
\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-1}
to the LHS and integrating from
0
to
t
, we get

\frac{1}{L-2}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}^{L-2}}-\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}}\right)\geq L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})t.

Using
\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}=1
, and simplifying the above equation we get

\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}\geq\frac{1}{1-tL(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})}.

The above equation implies that for some
T\leq 1/(L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0}))
,
\|{\mathbf{w}}(T)\|_{2}=\infty.
(We will show
T\geq 1/L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
later.)

We next derive related results for
{\mathbf{g}}(t)
. We know

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L},

which implies

\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-1}}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}}{dt}=L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}).

Integrating both sides from
0
to
t\in(0,T)
, we get

\frac{1}{L-2}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}^{L-2}}-\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}}\right)=\int_{0}^{t}L\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2})ds.

Using
\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}=1
and re-arranging the above equation gives us

1-\int_{0}^{t}L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2})ds=\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}}.

(36)

Substituting
{\mathbf{w}}(t)={\mathbf{g}}(t)/(T-t)^{1/(L-2)}
, we get

\frac{1-\int_{0}^{t}L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2})ds}{(T-t)}=\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}}.

Note that
\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{L-2}=T.
Next, we compute
\|{\mathbf{g}}(T)\|_{2}
. In the LHS of the above equality, at
t=T
, the denominator is obviously
0
, and the numerator is also
0
since, using
eq.

36
,

1-\int_{0}^{T}L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2})ds=\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}}=0.

Therefore, using L’Hopital’s rule,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{g}}(T)\|_{2}^{L-2}}=\frac{\lim_{t\rightarrow T}-L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2})}{-1}=\lim_{t\rightarrow T}L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}).

We will soon show that
\lim_{t\rightarrow T}{\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}={\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, which will complete the result for
\|{\mathbf{g}}(T)\|_{2}^{L-2}
. We now prove that
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}
is a decreasing function. For this, define

h(t):=\frac{1-\int_{0}^{t}L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(s)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2})ds}{(T-t)}=\frac{1-\int_{0}^{t}L(L-2)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}}(s))ds}{(T-t)}.

We next show that
h^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
, for all
t\in(0,T)
. This would imply
h(t)
is increasing and therefore,
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}
is decreasing, for all for all
t\in(0,T)
. For all
t\in[0,T)
,

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{-L(L-2)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}}(t))(T-t)+(1-\int_{0}^{t}L(L-2)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}}(s))ds)}{(T-t)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(T-t)}\left(-L(L-2)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{w}}(t))+h(t)\right).

Note that

h(0)=1/T\geq 0,\text{ and }h^{\prime}(0)=\frac{1}{T}\left(-L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})+\frac{1}{T}\right)\geq 0,

where the last inequality follows since
T\leq 1/(L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{0})).
For the sake of contradiction, suppose there exists a
t_{1}\in(0,T)
such that
h^{\prime}(t_{1})=-\epsilon
, for some
\epsilon>0
. Next, we compute
h^{\prime\prime}(t)
. Let
a(t)
denote the numerator of
h^{\prime}(t)
, then

a^{\prime}(t)=-L(L-2)\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t){\mathbf{w}}(t)^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}}+h^{\prime}(t).

Thus,

\displaystyle h^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{\prime}(t)}{(T-t)}+\frac{a(t)}{(T-t)^{2}}=\frac{1}{T-t}\left(a^{\prime}(t)+\frac{a(t)}{(T-t)}\right)=\frac{1}{T-t}\left(a^{\prime}(t)+h^{\prime}(t)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(T-t)}\left(-L(L-2)\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t){\mathbf{w}}(t)^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}}+2h^{\prime}(t)\right)

Since
-L(L-2)\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t){\mathbf{w}}(t)^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}(t))}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L}}\leq 0
, the above equation implies

h^{\prime\prime}(t)-\frac{2h^{\prime}(t)}{(T-t)}\leq 0.

From Lemma
30
, for any
t\in(t_{1},T)
, we get

h^{\prime}(t)\leq h^{\prime}(t_{1})/P(t),\text{ where }P(t)=e^{-\int_{t_{1}}^{t}2/(T-s)ds}=(T-t)^{2}/(T-t_{1})^{2}.

Hence, for any
t\in(t_{1},T)
,

h^{\prime}(t)\leq{-\epsilon(T-t_{1})^{2}}/(T-t)^{2}.

Integrating the above equation from
t_{1}
to
t\in(t_{1},T)
, we get

h(t)-h(t_{1})\leq-\epsilon(T-t_{1})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{T-t}-\frac{1}{T-t_{1}}\right).

Now, if
t
is chosen sufficiently close to
T
, then
h(t)
becomes negative. This is impossible since
h(t)=1/\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}
, leading to a contradiction. Hence,
h^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
, for all
t\in(0,T)
.

We next show that
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}>1-\gamma
, for all
t\in[0,T)
. For the sake of contradiction, suppose
\overline{T}\in[0,T)
is the smallest time such that
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})/\|{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}=1-\gamma.
Then, for all
t\in[0,\overline{T})
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}-L{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-2}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})-L{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-2}\frac{\Delta}{2}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-2}\Delta\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\geq 0,

(37)

where the first inequality follows from
eq.

34
. The above equation implies

\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}\right)\geq\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)>1-\gamma,

which leads to a contradiction. Now, from
(
Kumar and Haupt, 2025
, Lemma 2)
, we know

\lim_{t\to T}{{\mathbf{w}}(t)}/{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}=\overline{{\mathbf{w}}},

where
\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}
is a first-order KKT point of
eq.

4
. We next show that
\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}={\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. Since
\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}
is a first-order KKT point, from Lemma
21
, we know
L\mathcal{N}(\overline{{\mathbf{w}}})\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}(\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}).
Also, since
\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}^{*}\geq 1-\gamma
, from
eq.

34
, we have

0\leq{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}(\overline{{\mathbf{w}}})-L\mathcal{N}(\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}){\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}=-\frac{\Delta}{2}\|\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2},

implying
\overline{{\mathbf{w}}}={\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. Hence,
\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow T}\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}={1}/{(L(L-2)\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}))}\geq\|{\mathbf{g}}(0)\|_{2}^{L-2}=1/T.

Finally, since
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}
is a decreasing function,
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}=\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}^{L-2}/(T-t)\geq\|{\mathbf{g}}(T)\|_{2}^{L-2}/(T-t)
. Let
\alpha:=\|{\mathbf{g}}(T)\|_{2}^{L-2}
, then, using
eq.

37
, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\leq-\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{L-2}\Delta\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\leq-\frac{\alpha\Delta}{(T-t)}\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right).

Integrating the above equation from
0
to
t\in(0,T)
, we get

\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)\leq\left(1-\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\alpha\Delta}{(T-s)}ds}\leq\gamma\left(1-\frac{t}{T}\right)^{\alpha\Delta},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 27
.

Let
{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Then, for any fixed
\widetilde{T}>0
, there exists a
\widetilde{C}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\psi}(t,{\mathbf{p}})-\bm{\psi}(t,{\mathbf{q}})\|_{2}\leq\widetilde{C}\|{\mathbf{p}}-{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2},\text{for all }t\in[-\widetilde{T},\widetilde{T}].

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)=\bm{\psi}(t,{\mathbf{p}})
and
{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)=\bm{\psi}(t,{\mathbf{q}})
. Then,

\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}_{1}=-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}),{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(0)={\mathbf{p}},\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}_{2}=-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}),{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(0)={\mathbf{q}}.

Since
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
has locally Lipshitz gradient, and
\widetilde{T}
is fixed, we may assume that there exists a
\mu
such that

\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t))-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t))\|_{2}\leq\mu\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2},\text{ for all }t\in[-\widetilde{T},\widetilde{T}].

Hence, for any
t\in[0,\widetilde{T}]
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}^{2}=-({\mathbf{u}}_{1}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2})^{\top}(\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{1})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}))\leq\mu\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}^{2}.

Integrating the above inequality from
0
to
t\in[0,\widetilde{T}]
, we get

\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}\leq e^{\mu t}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(0)\|_{2}\leq e^{\mu\widetilde{T}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(0)\|_{2}.

(38)

Similarly, for any
t\in[-\widetilde{T},0]
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}^{2}=-({\mathbf{u}}_{1}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2})^{\top}(\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{1})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}))\geq-\mu\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}^{2}.

Integrating the above inequality from
t\in[-\widetilde{T},0]
to
0
, we get

\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}\leq e^{-\mu t}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(0)\|_{2}\leq e^{\mu\widetilde{T}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}(0)\|_{2}.

(39)

Combining
eq.

38
and
eq.

39
completes the proof.
∎

A.3
Auxiliary Lemmata

In this subsection, we provide auxiliary lemmata which are crucial for the proofs.

Lemma 28
.

Suppose
\eta_{1}>\eta_{2}>0
, then
f_{1}(t)=\frac{1-\eta_{1}t}{1-\eta_{2}t}
and
f_{2}(t)=\frac{1}{1-\eta_{1}t}
are monotonically decreasing and increasing functions, respectively, on
t\in[0,1/\eta_{1})
.

Proof.

The claim follows since, for
t\in[0,1/\eta_{1})
,

f_{1}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{-\eta_{1}(1-\eta_{2}t)+\eta_{2}(1-\eta_{1}t)}{(1-\eta_{2}t)^{2}}=\frac{\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}}{(1-\eta_{2}t)^{2}}\leq 0,\text{ and }f_{2}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{\eta_{1}}{(1-\eta_{1}t)^{2}}\geq 0.

∎

Lemma 29
.

If
g(t)=b/(1-at)
, for some
a>0
. Then, for all
t\in[0,1/a)
,
e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}=(1-at)^{b/a}.

Proof.

Since
\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds={-b}\ln(1-at)/a,
we have
e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}=(1-at)^{b/a}.

∎

Lemma 30
.

If
x(t)
satisfies
\dot{x}\leq g(t)x+h(t),
then

x(t)\leq\frac{1}{P(t)}\left(x(0)+\int_{0}^{t}P(s)h(s)ds\right),\text{ where }P(t)=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}.

Proof.

Multiplying by
e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}
on both sides, we get

e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}h(t)\geq e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}\dot{x}-e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}g(t)x=\frac{d}{dt}\left(e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}x\right).

Let
P(t)=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g(s)ds}
, then integrating both sides from
0
to
t
, we get

\int_{0}^{t}P(s)h(s)ds\geq P(t)x(t)-x(0),

which can be rearranged to get the desired result.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs Omitted from
Section

3.1

This section contains the proof of Lemma
8
,
9
and
10
, which were used to prove Theorem
4
.

Proof.

of Lemma
10
:

We choose
\gamma>0
sufficiently small such that for all unit-norm vector
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\text{ and }

(40)

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)^{\top}\left(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2},\forall t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0.

(41)

The first inequality follows from Lemma
24
. The second inequality holds since, from Lemma
21
,
\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|_{2}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
, and
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is continuous in the neighborhood of
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. The third inequality follows from Lemma
23
.

We further define
f({\mathbf{w}})=\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))
. Then, note that

\|f({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq\|\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\|\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})\|_{2}\leq Kn\|\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\|\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2},

where the first inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and the second follows from the smoothness of the loss function (see
Assumption

2
). Note that the RHS in the above inequality is
3-
homogeneous in
{\mathbf{w}}
. Thus, there exists a
\beta>0
such that

\|f({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq\beta\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3},\text{ for all }{\mathbf{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}.

(42)

Also,
f({\mathbf{w}})
is continuously differentiable in the neighborhood of
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. Thus, we may assume that for all vector
{\mathbf{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
that satisfy
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma,
we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla f({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})\right\|_{2}\left|\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}}),y_{i})-\ell^{\prime}({0},y_{i})\right|+

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})\nabla\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left|\ell^{\prime\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}}),y_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}|\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})|+\|\nabla\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})\nabla\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{x}}_{i};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}\|_{2},

where the second inequality uses smoothness of the loss function. Note that the final upper bound in the above equation is
2-
homogeneous. Thus, there exists a
\zeta>0
such that for all vectors
{\mathbf{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
that satisfy
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}/\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma,
we have

\|\nabla f({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq\zeta\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Furthermore, from the mean value theorem, we have

\|f({\mathbf{w}}_{1})-f({\mathbf{w}}_{2})\|_{2}\leq\|\nabla f(\tilde{{\mathbf{w}}})\|_{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2}\leq\zeta\max(\|{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2},\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2})\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2},

(43)

where
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{1}/\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}\|_{2},{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{2}/\|{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma.

Now, let
{\mathbf{q}}(t)=\bm{\psi}\left(t,{{\mathbf{a}}}_{\delta}\right)
,
{\mathbf{u}}(t)=\bm{\psi}\left(t,\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)
, then
\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}=\left\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}-\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\delta^{3},
which implies
\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}\leq\delta+C_{1}\delta^{3}
. Therefore, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(0)+{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}({\mathbf{q}}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}(0))}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta-\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)-{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\geq\frac{\delta-C_{1}\delta^{3}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}_{\delta}(0)\|_{2}}\geq\frac{\delta-C_{1}\delta^{3}}{\delta+C_{1}\delta^{3}}>1-\gamma.

Define

\overline{T}_{1}=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}=1-\gamma\right\}.

Recall that
{\mathbf{q}}(t)
satisfies

\dot{{\mathbf{q}}}=-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}})^{\top}\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}}),{\mathbf{y}})=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}})-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})).

Multiplying the above equation by
{\mathbf{q}}^{\top}
from the left we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}})-2\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{q}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}})=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}}/\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2})\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{2},

where the first equality follows from Lemma
20
, and the first inequality is due to convexity of the loss function. The second equality holds since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}})
is two-homogeneous.

Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma
for all
t\in[0,\overline{T}_{1}]
, from
eq.

40
, we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{2},

which implies

\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\text{ for all }t\in[0,\overline{T}_{1}].

(44)

Let
{\mathbf{z}}(t)=e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, which is the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}),{\mathbf{z}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{*}.

Note that, for
t\in[0,\overline{T}_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}(t))\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)-\nabla\mathcal{N}\left({{\mathbf{z}}(t)}\right)\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}-\|{\mathbf{z}}(t)\|_{2}\frac{{\mathbf{z}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{z}}(t)\|_{2}}\right\|^{2}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}(t)}\right\|^{2},

(45)

where the inequality follows from
{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}/{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}=\|{\mathbf{z}}(t)\|_{2}
,
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma
for all
t\in[0,\overline{T}_{1}]
, and
eq.

41
. Hence, for
t\in[0,\overline{T}_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\dot{{\mathbf{q}}}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\nabla\mathcal{N}({{\mathbf{q}}})}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-\nabla\mathcal{N}({{\mathbf{z}}})\right)-\left(\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right)^{\top}\frac{f({\mathbf{q}})}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}(t)}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\beta\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}(t)}\right\|_{2}\frac{\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{3}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}},

where the inequality follows from
eq.

45
and
eq.

42
. The above equation implies

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}+\frac{\beta\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{3}}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}.

Using Lemma
30
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\left(\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(0)\right\|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\beta\|{\mathbf{q}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\left(\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(0)\right\|_{2}+\beta\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{4s\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{2}\delta^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},

where the second equality uses
eq.

44
. In the third inequality,
C_{2}
is some sufficiently large constant, and it follows since, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
,
\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}=O(\delta)
and

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{q}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(0)\right\|_{2}=\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}-\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}\|_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}\|_{2}}\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}-\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+\left|\delta-\|{\mathbf{a}}_{\delta}\|_{2}\right|}{\delta-C_{1}\delta^{3}}\leq\frac{2C_{1}\delta^{3}}{\delta-C_{1}\delta^{3}}=O(\delta^{2}).

Define
\tau_{q}(t)\coloneqq{\mathbf{q}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)
, then, using the definition of
\overline{T}_{1}
, we have

1-\gamma=\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(\overline{T}_{1})}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(\overline{T}_{1})\|_{2}}=\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}(\overline{T}_{1})+{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\tau_{q}(\overline{T}_{1})}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(\overline{T}_{1})\|_{2}/\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}}\geq\frac{e^{2\overline{T}_{1}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}-\|\tau_{q}(\overline{T}_{1})\|_{2}}{e^{2\overline{T}_{1}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\geq 1-{C_{2}^{2}\delta^{2}e^{4\overline{T}_{1}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}},

which implies
\overline{T}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{C_{2}\delta}\right).

Next, we focus on
{\mathbf{u}}(t)
. Note that
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(0)/\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}=1>1-\gamma
, and
{\mathbf{u}}(t)
satisfies

\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}=-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}})^{\top}\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{y}})=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}})-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})).

Thus, if we define

\overline{T}_{2}=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}=1-\gamma\right\},

then, similar to
{\mathbf{q}}(t)
, we can show, for all
t\in[0,\overline{T}_{2}]
,

\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}.

Also, there exists a sufficiently large constant
C_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\left(\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(0)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(0)\right\|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\beta\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}^{2}\delta^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},

and
\overline{T}_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{C_{3}\delta}\right).

Next, choose
C_{4}>0
sufficiently small such that
C_{4}<\sqrt{\gamma}\min(1/C_{2},1/C_{3})
and
C_{4}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})/(\zeta(1+C_{1})^{2})
. Then, for all
t\in[0,\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{C_{4}}{\delta}\right)]
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-\gamma,\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{q}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-\gamma,\text{ and }

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\leq C_{4},\|{\mathbf{q}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{q}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\leq C_{4}(1+C_{1}\delta^{2}).

(46)

Therefore, for
t\in\left[0,\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{C_{4}}{\delta}\right)\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}-{\mathbf{q}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left({{\mathbf{u}}}-{\mathbf{q}}\right)^{\top}\left({\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}}-\dot{{\mathbf{q}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left({{\mathbf{u}}}-{\mathbf{q}}\right)^{\top}\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{q}})\right)-\left({{\mathbf{u}}}-{\mathbf{q}}\right)^{\top}\left(f({\mathbf{u}})-f({\mathbf{q}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}}-{\mathbf{q}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\zeta\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}}-{\mathbf{q}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\max(\|{\mathbf{q}}\|_{2}^{2},\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{2}).

\displaystyle\leq(3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})+\zeta C_{4}^{2}(1+C_{1})^{2})\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}-{\mathbf{q}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}-{\mathbf{q}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2},

where the first inequality follows from combining mean value theorem with
eq.

40
and
0
-homogeneity of
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
, and
eq.

43
. The second inequality follows from
eq.

46
when
\delta\leq 1
. The last inequality uses our assumption on
C_{4}.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbf{u}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{C_{4}}{\delta}\right)\right)-{\mathbf{q}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{C_{4}}{\delta}\right)\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)-{\mathbf{q}}(0)\right\|_{2}e^{\frac{4\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{C_{4}}{\delta}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}C_{4}^{2}\delta.

Since
\widetilde{T}
is fixed, from Lemma
27
and the above inequality, there exists a
{C}>0
such that for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right),{{\mathbf{a}}}_{\delta}\right)-\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right),\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\delta,

for all
t\in[-\widetilde{T},\widetilde{T}]
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof.

of Lemma
8
:

We choose
\gamma>0
sufficiently small such that for all unit-norm vectors
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma
, we have
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
and

\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)^{\top}\left(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2},\forall t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0.

(47)

The first and second inequality follow from Lemma
24
and Lemma
23
, respectively. Let
{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(t)=\bm{\psi}\left(t,\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\text{ and }{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{2}}(t)=\bm{\psi}\left(t,\delta_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right),
where recall that
\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{1}>0
. Let
{\mathbf{z}}(t)=e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, which is the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}),{\mathbf{z}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{*}.

Note that
{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(0)=\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. Define

T^{*}_{1}=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(t)\|_{2}}=1-\gamma\right\}.

Then, as demonstrated in the proof of Lemma
10
, we can show

\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}.

Also, there exists a sufficiently large constant
C_{2}
such that

\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}\leq C_{2}^{2}\delta_{1}^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\text{ for all }t\in[0,T_{1}^{*}],

(48)

and
{T}_{1}^{*}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{C_{2}\delta_{1}}\right)
. Hence, if
\delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\gamma}/C_{2}
, then
{T}_{1}^{*}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right)
.

Next, since
\delta_{1}{\mathbf{z}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right)\right)=\delta_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, using
eq.

48
we get

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}\left(\frac{\ln\left({\delta_{2}}/{\delta_{1}}\right)}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\right)-\delta_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right)\right)-\delta_{1}{\mathbf{z}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right)\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}^{2}\delta_{1}^{3}\delta_{2}^{3}}{\delta_{1}^{3}}=C_{2}\delta_{2}^{3}.

(49)

Now, note that

\displaystyle\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{1}}\right),\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right),\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\right),\bm{\psi}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right),\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\right),{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}\right)\right)\right),

Combining the above equality with
eq.

49
and Lemma
10
, we get that for any fixed
t\in(-\infty,\infty)
and all sufficiently small
\delta_{2},\delta_{1}>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\left\|\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{1}}\right),\delta_{1}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)-\bm{\psi}\left(t+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\right),\delta_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\delta_{2},

which implies
{\mathbf{p}}(t)
exists for all
t\in(-\infty,\infty)
.

We next prove that
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{p}}(0))\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})-\eta
, for some
\eta>0
. Let
{\mathbf{u}}(t)=\bm{\psi}(t,\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*})
and
{\mathbf{z}}(t)=e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, then from
eq.

48
, there exists a sufficiently large constant
B_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}/{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}(t)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq B_{1}^{2}\delta^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\text{ for all }t\in[0,T_{1}],

(50)

where
{T}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{B_{1}\delta}\right).

Define
\alpha\coloneqq\max_{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}=1}\|\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}
. From the convexity of the loss function, we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})^{\top}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))^{\top}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}})+\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})^{\top}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+Kn\|\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+\alpha^{2}Kn\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{4}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}),

(51)

where the last two inequalities follow from smoothness of the loss function and the definition of
\alpha
. Now, choose
\epsilon\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that

\epsilon\leq\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{B_{1}},\text{ and }Kn\alpha^{2}(\sqrt{\epsilon}+\epsilon^{2.5}/B_{1}^{2})^{4}\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\frac{\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{2},\text{ if }\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}B_{1}^{2},

where the second inequality holds true since
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is continuous and
\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})>0
. Note that

{T}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{B_{1}\delta}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\ln\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\delta}\right)\coloneqq T_{2}.

Hence, using
eq.

50
,
\|{\mathbf{u}}(T_{2})\|_{2}\leq\epsilon+B_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{3}
. Also, if we define
\tau\coloneqq{\mathbf{u}}(T_{2})-\delta{\mathbf{z}}(T_{2})
, then
\|\tau\|_{2}\leq B_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{3}
. Therefore, using
eq.

51
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{u}}(T_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+\alpha^{2}Kn\|{\mathbf{u}}(T_{2})\|_{2}^{4}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}(T_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+\alpha^{2}Kn(\epsilon+B_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{3})^{4}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}(T_{2}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})+\epsilon^{2}(\alpha^{2}Kn(\sqrt{\epsilon}+B_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2.5})^{4}-\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}(T_{2})/\epsilon))\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})-\epsilon^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})/2,

where the last inequality follows from the choice of
\epsilon
and since
\|{\mathbf{u}}(T_{2})/\epsilon-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}\leq B_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2}.
If we define
\eta=\epsilon^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})/2
, then the above equation implies, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
,

\mathcal{L}\left(\bm{\psi}\left(\frac{\ln(\epsilon)+\ln\left({1}/{\delta}\right)}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right)=\mathcal{L}\left(\bm{\psi}\left(\frac{\ln\left({\epsilon}/{\delta}\right)}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right)\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})-\eta,

where note that
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and fixed, and thus,
\eta
is fixed. Since
\ln(\epsilon)<0
, and the loss decreases with time, the above equation implies, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
,

\mathcal{L}\left(\bm{\psi}\left(\frac{\ln\left({1}/{\delta}\right)}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right)\leq\mathcal{L}\left(\bm{\psi}\left(\frac{\ln\left({\epsilon}/{\delta}\right)}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\right)\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})-\eta.

Taking
\delta\to 0
, gives us
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{p}}(0))\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{0})-\eta,
which completes the proof.
∎

Proof.

of Lemma
9
:

We choose
\gamma>0
sufficiently small such that for all unit-norm vectors
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfying
{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\leq\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*}),\text{ and }

(52)

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}})-\nabla\mathcal{N}(t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*})\right)^{\top}\left(t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right)\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|t_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-t_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2},\forall t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0.

(53)

The first inequality follows from Lemma
24
. The second inequality holds since, from Lemma
21
,
\|\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|_{2}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
, and
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is continuous in the neighborhood of
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
. The third inequality follows from Lemma
23
. Define
f({\mathbf{w}})=\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))
, then, as shown in the proof of Lemma
10
, there exists a
\beta>0
such that

\|f({\mathbf{w}})\|_{2}\leq\beta\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3},\text{ for all }{\mathbf{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}.

(54)

Let
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)
denote the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}),{\mathbf{z}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{0}.

Since
{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})
, it follows that
{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)
converges to
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
in direction. Thus, we can assume there exists some time
T_{\gamma}
and a constant
B_{1}
such that

\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}}=1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{8},\text{ and }\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\|_{2}\leq B_{1},\text{ for all }t\in[0,T_{\gamma}].

Let
\mu_{1}\coloneqq\max_{\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}=1}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}),
and
{\mathbf{w}}(t)\coloneqq\bm{\psi}\left(t,\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\right),
then
{\mathbf{w}}(t)
is the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})),{\mathbf{w}}(0)=\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0}.

Multiplying the above equation by
{\mathbf{w}}^{\top}
from the left and using convexity of
\ell(\cdot,\cdot)
, we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})-2\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{w}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})\leq\mu_{1}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2},

which implies
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\delta e^{\mu_{1}t}
. Now, since
\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is locally Lipschitz, and for all
t\in[0,T_{\gamma}]
,
\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\|_{2}\leq B_{1}
and
\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{2}/\delta\leq e^{\mu_{1}T_{\gamma}}:=B_{2}
, there exists a
\mu_{2}>0
such that

\|\nabla\mathcal{N}({{\mathbf{w}}(t)}/{\delta})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t))\|_{2}\leq\mu_{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)/\delta-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\|_{2},\forall t\in[0,T_{\gamma}].

Hence, for all
t\in[0,T_{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\dot{{\mathbf{w}}}}{\delta}-\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}\right)^{\top}\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({{\mathbf{w}}}/{\delta})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{1})\right)-\left(\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}\right)^{\top}\frac{f({\mathbf{w}})}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{2}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\beta\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3}/\delta,

which implies

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(t)}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{2}\leq\mu_{2}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{2}+\beta\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{3}/\delta.

Using Lemma
30
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})}{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\mu_{2}T_{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T_{\gamma}}e^{-\mu_{2}s}\beta\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\delta ds

\displaystyle\leq e^{\mu_{2}T_{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T_{\gamma}}\beta\|{\mathbf{w}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\delta ds\leq\beta\delta^{2}e^{\mu_{2}T_{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T_{\gamma}}e^{3\mu_{1}s}ds\leq A_{1}\delta^{2},

(55)

where
A_{1}
is a sufficiently large constant. Let
\tau_{1}\coloneqq{{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})}/{\delta}-{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})
, then, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}}=\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})+{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\tau_{1}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}/\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1-\gamma^{2}/8)\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}-A_{1}\delta^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}/\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1-\gamma^{2}/8)\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}-A_{1}\delta^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}+A_{1}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\gamma^{2}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}/8-2A_{1}\delta^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}+A_{1}\delta^{2}}\geq 1-\gamma^{2}/4.

Let
A_{2}=\|{\mathbf{z}}_{1}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}
, where note that
A_{2}
is a constant that does not depend on
\delta
. Define
{\mathbf{w}}_{1}={\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})/\|{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}
and
\widetilde{\delta}=\|{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma})\|_{2}
. From
eq.

55
, we know

\widetilde{\delta}\in(A_{2}\delta-A_{1}\delta^{3},A_{2}\delta+A_{1}\delta^{3}),

thus,
\widetilde{\delta}
can be made sufficiently small by choosing
\delta
sufficiently small. Next, let

{\mathbf{u}}(t)=\bm{\psi}(t+T_{\gamma},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0})
. Then,

{\mathbf{u}}(t)=\bm{\psi}(t+T_{\gamma},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0})=\bm{\psi}(t,\bm{\psi}(T_{\gamma},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0}))=\bm{\psi}(t,{\mathbf{w}}(T_{\gamma}))=\bm{\psi}(t,\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{w}}_{1}),

and
{\mathbf{u}}(t)
is the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}})-\mathcal{J}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}})),{\mathbf{u}}(0)=\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{w}}_{1}.

Since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(0)/\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}={\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{1}\geq 1-\gamma^{2}/4>1-\gamma
, we define

T^{*}=\min_{t\geq 0}\left\{t:\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}=1-\gamma\right\}.

Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma
, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, from
eq.

52
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}^{2}}{dt}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}})-2\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}})^{\top}(\ell^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{X}};{\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{y}})-\ell^{\prime}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{y}}))\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}^{2},

which implies

\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}=\widetilde{\delta}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}.

Next, let
{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)=e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}
, which is the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}),{\mathbf{z}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{*}.

(56)

Note that, for
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}\right)-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t))\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}\right)-\nabla\mathcal{N}\left({{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)}\right)\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}-\|{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}\frac{{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}}\right\|^{2}=2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}-{{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)}\right\|^{2},

(57)

where the inequality follows from
{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}/{\|{\mathbf{u}}(0)\|_{2}}\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}=\|{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\|_{2}
,
{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)/\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}\geq 1-\gamma
for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, and
eq.

53
. Then, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right)^{\top}\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}/\widetilde{\delta})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{2})\right)-\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right)^{\top}\frac{f({\mathbf{u}})}{\widetilde{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\beta\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right\|_{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta}.

From the above equation, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}\right\|_{2}+\beta\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta},

which implies

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\left(\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\beta\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+\beta\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\int_{0}^{t}e^{4s\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}ds

\displaystyle\leq e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+{A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}},

where
A_{3}
is a sufficiently large constant. Now, define
\overline{T}=\frac{4\ln(1/\widetilde{\delta})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}
, then we claim that
\overline{T}<T^{*}
, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
. For the sake of contradiction, let
T^{*}\leq\overline{T}
. Define
\tau_{2}(t)={{\mathbf{u}}(t)}-\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(t)
, then

\|\tau_{2}(T^{*})\|_{2}=\|{{\mathbf{u}}(T^{*})}-\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{2}(T^{*})\|_{2}\leq\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{3}e^{6T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}.

Dividing by
\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}
on both sides and using
T^{*}\leq\overline{T}
, we get

\frac{\|\tau_{2}(T^{*})\|_{2}}{\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\leq\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}+{A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{4\overline{T}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Next, by definition of
T^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\gamma=\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(T^{*})}{\|{\mathbf{u}}(T^{*})\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(T^{*})}{\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}=\frac{\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}+{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}\tau_{2}(T^{*})}{\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\geq 1-\frac{\|\tau_{2}(T^{*})\|_{2}}{\widetilde{\delta}e^{2T^{*}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Now, since
{\mathbf{w}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma^{2}/4
,

\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}=2-2{\mathbf{w}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\leq\gamma^{2}/2.

Hence,

\displaystyle 1-\gamma\geq 1-\gamma/\sqrt{2}-{A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{4\overline{T}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}=1-\gamma/\sqrt{2}-{A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}}/{\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{16\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}},

which implies

\gamma(1-1/\sqrt{2})\leq{A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}}/{\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{16\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}}=A_{3}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{2\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

The above inequality can not be true if
\delta
is chosen sufficiently small, leading to a contradiction. Hence,
\overline{T}<T^{*}
.
Next, let
{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)
be the the solution of

\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}=\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}),{\mathbf{z}}(0)={\mathbf{w}}_{1},

(58)

Since
{\mathbf{w}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\geq 1-\gamma^{2}/4>1-\gamma
, then we may assume without loss of generality that
\gamma>0
is sufficiently small such that Lemma
25
is applicable and hence,

\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-e^{-t\Delta}\gamma,\text{ for all }t\geq 0,\text{ and }{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)={\mathbf{g}}(t)e^{2t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},

(59)

where
\|{\mathbf{g}}(t)\|_{2}\in[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
, for some
\kappa_{2}\geq\kappa_{1}>0
. By definition of
\overline{T}
, we have

\frac{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}\geq 1-\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{4\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\gamma,\text{ and }\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}=\frac{\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}{\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}},

which implies

\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}-{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}=2-\frac{2{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})}{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}}\leq 2\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{4\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\gamma.

Multiplying by
\widetilde{\delta}^{2}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}^{2}
on both sides gives us

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})-\widetilde{\delta}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\widetilde{\delta}^{2}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}^{2}\delta^{\frac{4\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\gamma

\displaystyle\leq{2\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}^{2}\gamma}\frac{\widetilde{\delta}^{2}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{4\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}}{\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{16\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}}=2{\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}^{2}\gamma}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{6\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

(60)

Next, note that for
t\in[0,\overline{T}]
,
{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)}/{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\|_{2}},{{\mathbf{w}}_{*}^{\top}{\mathbf{u}}(t)}/{\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}}\geq 1-\gamma
. Since
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}})
is
0
-homogeneous, from the mean value theorem and
eq.

52
, for all
t\in[0,\overline{T}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right)^{\top}\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}(t)/\widetilde{\delta})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t))\right)\right\|_{2}\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Hence, for
t\in[0,\overline{T}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\dot{{\mathbf{u}}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-\dot{{\mathbf{z}}}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}\right)^{\top}\left(\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{u}}/\widetilde{\delta})-\nabla\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{z}}_{3})\right)-\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}\right)^{\top}\frac{f({\mathbf{w}})}{\widetilde{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}+\beta\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta}.

From the above equation, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}\leq 3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}+\beta\|{\mathbf{u}}(t)\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta},

which implies

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{u}}(t)}{\widetilde{\delta}}-{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(t)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})s}\beta\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{2}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta}ds

\displaystyle\leq\beta\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{3\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})s}ds\leq{{A_{4}}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}e^{6t\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}},

where
A_{4}
is a sufficiently large constant. Putting
t=\overline{T}
in the above equation gives us

\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(\overline{T})}-\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\right\|_{2}\leq A_{4}\widetilde{\delta}^{3}e^{6\overline{T}\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}=A_{4}\widetilde{\delta}^{3}/\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{24\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}=A_{4}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{3\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

From
eq.

60
and the above equation, we get

\displaystyle\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(\overline{T})}-\widetilde{\delta}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}(\overline{T})}-\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\delta}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}-\widetilde{\delta}{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(A_{4}+{\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\sqrt{2\gamma}}\right)\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{3\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Also, note that

\widetilde{\delta}\|{\mathbf{z}}_{3}(\overline{T})\|_{2}=\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}{\widetilde{\delta}}/{\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}}=\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Thus, using the above two equations and the definition of
{\mathbf{u}}(t)
, we get

\left\|\bm{\psi}(\overline{T}+T_{\gamma},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0})-\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(A_{4}+{\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\sqrt{2\gamma}}\right)\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{3\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}.

Since
\overline{T}
depends on
\delta
,
\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}
may also depend on
\delta
, but
\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\in[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
. Hence, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
,

\left\|\bm{\psi}(\overline{T}+T_{\gamma},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0})-\|{\mathbf{g}}(\overline{T})\|_{2}\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}{\mathbf{w}}_{*}\right\|_{2}\leq C\widetilde{\delta}^{\frac{3\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}},

where
C
is a sufficiently large constant, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof.

of Corollary
5
:
Since
{\mathbf{p}}(t)
is bounded for all
t\geq 0
, there exists a constant
B>0
such that
\|{\mathbf{p}}(t)\|_{2}\leq B
, for all
t\geq 0
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
has locally Lipschitz gradient, there exists a constant
\widetilde{A}>0
such that, if
\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}\|_{2},\|{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2}\leq 2B
, then

\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{w}}_{1})-\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{w}}_{2})\|_{2}\leq\widetilde{A}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{2}.

(61)

Since
{\mathbf{p}}^{*}=\lim_{t\to\infty}{\mathbf{p}}(t)
and
\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{p}}^{*})=\mathbf{0}
, for any
\epsilon\in(0,B)
, there exists a
T_{\epsilon}
such that

\|{\mathbf{p}}(T_{\epsilon})-{\mathbf{p}}_{*}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon/2\text{ and }\|\nabla\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{p}}(T_{\epsilon}))\|_{2}\leq\epsilon/2.

(62)

Since
T_{\epsilon}
does not depend on
\delta
, therefore, from Theorem
4
, for all sufficiently small
\delta>0
and for all
t\in[-T_{\epsilon},T_{\epsilon}]
,

\left\|\bm{\psi}\left(t+T_{1}+\frac{\ln(1/b_{\delta})}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}+\frac{\ln({1}/{\widetilde{\delta}})}{2\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\right)-{\mathbf{p}}(t)\right\|_{2}\leq\widetilde{C}\delta^{\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+8\mathcal{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{*})}}\leq\epsilon/2.

Putting
t=T_{\epsilon}
in the above equation, and using
eq.

62
, we get

\left\|\bm{\psi}\left(T_{\delta},\delta{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\right)-{\mathbf{p}}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon,

where
